# Board — open work and waiting decisions

The one canonical list. `docs/status.md` describes the state of the
software and points here; this file holds what is owed, by whom, and what
Alexa has not yet decided. An item leaves this file only by being done,
refused or superseded, with the date; a session ending is not a reason.

Format of a row: stable id, type, dated origin, owner, state, next action,
dependency, evidence. States: `waiting` (Alexa), `owed` (agreed, not
started), `open` (not yet agreed or sized), `active` (an agent holds it),
`blocked` (needs something outside the project), `done`, `refused`.
`scripts/check-docs.sh` checks the section names and the row shape.

| ORCH-022 | **May a runner silent for `lost_after` keep its link?** The last of the three known-unstable tests, and the only one left: **V3**, `herd-hub/tests/slots.rs` `a_runner_killed_mid_job_gives_its_slot_back` (`async fn` at `:205`, the failing assertion at `:292-295`). V4 (`follower_alive`) was fixed in batch 9 and the third sighting (`durability.rs:701`) was a test-isolation `flock` race, so this row is now one question and not three tests. **The cause is in the product and is located exactly** (D228): `one_watchdog_pass` **deletes a runner from its peers map before asking that runner what it holds**, so D169's word-over-clock rule is void in precisely the case D169 was written for — a runner descheduled briefly under load, whose word would have been `StillRunningIt`. **The obvious fix does not work and is withdrawn:** an agent wrote the ask-before-dropping reorder, proved it red-before and green-after with a deterministic unit test, ran V3's own acceptance regime and got **5 of 5 red, unchanged**. The drop and the write-off are **100 ms apart, one watchdog tick**, so asking first only moves the clock's decision to the next pass; and V3 asserts the queue reason says *"build has 1 slot(s) and 1 are taken"* while what it gets is *"no runner is connected to this hub"* — the fleet reads **empty**, not busy, so a fix aimed at the attempt cannot move it | CI run 40114565027, 2026-10-24; widened by `00-baseline.md §8`, 2026-10-25; narrowed to one question and moved here from *Open* by `herd-merger` at batch 11's landing, 2026-10-30, on the orchestrator's instruction | Alexa | waiting | **The test is what should move.** A machine silent for `lost_after` *is* lost, the product is doing deliberately what the test complains of, and V3's 2200 ms starvation is a faithful scaling of the promise rather than a counter-example to it. This is the orchestrator's recommendation and it is **not** a decision: E-21 is a published promise, so the reading is Alexa's | **Give the link its own bound** — `lost_after * 2`, which must stay above `lost_after / 2` so `register_runner`'s displacement of a quiet incumbent still comes first. It makes V3 holdable, **and it costs the promise**: E-21 says a runner nothing has been heard from for about 60 s is lost and its jobs attempted once more, and this doubles that to about **two minutes** for a machine genuinely off | **D228**, **D169**; E-21 in `docs/coverage.md`; `herd-hub/src/server.rs` `one_watchdog_pass`, `what_a_runner_says`; `herd-hub/tests/slots.rs:205`, `:292-295`; `history/2026-10-29-orch022-v3.md` (the cause, quoted from `1a8c1ae`); `history/2026-10-29-orch-197-record-scan.md` *Risks and findings* 1 and *Decisions needed* (the withdrawn patch and its 5-of-5-red measurement); `history/2026-10-29-v3v4-rates.md` (the rate tables, the kept probe at `V3_STARVE=2200`); `history/2026-10-30-merge-batch-11.md` |

| ORCH-215 | **`packs/` has no retention, no sweep and no budget. Who owns its growth, and what is the policy?** The hub's pack registry creates one directory per `(commit, key)` under `packs/manifests/<id>/` — `manifest.tsv`, `pack.meta` and `registered.json`, kilobytes each — and **nothing in Herd ever removes one**: `docs/retention` and the output budget govern `files/` and not this, so the registry only grows, unbounded in count, in a directory this layout move introduces. Four Rust gates plus `check-docs.sh` and `check-orchestration.sh` run on the merge result; merged under ORCH-001 on green CI. **ORCH-083's last open dependency is cleared by this merge.** Built on `orch-081-hub-stop-path`: `StopSignals` installed before the state root is opened; `State::stopping` refuses submissions before anything is minted and starts no further pass; `let_the_pass_finish` waits for the claim lock up to `STOP_WITHIN`; exit 0 clean, non-zero on overrun. **`STOP_WITHIN = 5 s` is recorded as D184**, allocated at this integration — `07` V9's figure, justified against `11`'s 3.128 s ext4 pass at `N` = 1 000 and **not** holding at `08`'s 9.970 s ZFS pass; an overrun is a **crash and not a corruption**, and the hub exits non-zero on one so a stop that is still a crash stays countable; five fits inside docker compose's ten-second default grace, which would otherwise `SIGKILL` the stop path mid-drain. **Alexa was asked whether to record the bound and had not answered when D184 was written**, which D184 states, so he may overturn it; the constant's doc comment now points at it. | ORCH-107 | **The suite's host-contention flakiness, which is not ORCH-069 and has had no row.** On this workstation under several agents, the real-hub tests time out rather than assert anything wrong: `real_herd/mod.rs:649` (`herdctl … never came back`, the 30 s `PATIENCE`) and `:789` (`wait_until`). At PR #93's integration, 2026-10-27, five targets gave **21 failures, nineteen of them these two timeouts**, with the host at a load between 2 and 19 and three full suites running at once; ORCH-081's agent hit the same at load 20.62. **Two agents have now each spent time proving those failures were not theirs.** **And part of it is a trap in how the suite is run, not load alone, which is the first thing to check when sizing this.** `./scripts/test.sh` run without `cargo build --workspace --all-targets` first makes the `real_herd` tests **build the binaries they drive inside their own `PATIENCE`**, so the build eats the timeout. ORCH-092's author got 7, then 3, then 2 failing targets on identical code before working it out; `herd-merger` then saw the same shape across four integrations on 2026-10-27 — **three targets red without the prebuild, two with it, four runs in a row**, the target that drops out each time being `killed_hub` with a `:649` timeout. | ORCH-115 | **A derived build can be inadmissible by construction, and the symptom is a hang rather than a wrong figure.** A build's admitted memory is its derived `-j` times its per-thread figure (`herd-hub/src/matcher.rs:1534`), while the `-j` is three quarters of the **lend** divided by that same figure, and the admission it is weighed against is the lend **less the runner's own floor**. So there are two sightings now, one local under induced load and one on a GitHub runner at rest, which is what makes contention rather than a wrong value the thing to test **Diagnosed and fixed (test), landed in batch 2 as D195.** It is not a lost link: `herd_runner::take_job` has **two** refusals that set `holding`, and the assertion pinned one of them. `NotTaken::StillRunningThisJob` (D182, one machine runs one job once) is reachable **only below** the ceiling, so it reports a figure under it by construction. That quantity is **how many of its originals the runner still holds when the second attempts arrive**, and what moved it was never the clock. Measured on an instrumented copy with wall-clock stamps: the test cut the link **one millisecond** after the hub's second dispatch, so an assignment the runner had not yet read went down with the link and left the machine holding one original or none. Fixed on `orch-119-clockless-oracle` by **building the premise instead of timing it** (D208): the jobs are held open on a release file, as `admission.rs` does, and the link is cut on `JobState::Running` — the runner's own word, written from inside the lock that counts the ceiling, `herd-hub/src/server.rs:4144` — instead of `JobState::Assigned`, which says only that a frame was sent. **The fix removes the regime and widens no timeout:** `CEILING`, `THE_OUTAGE` and `SLEEP_ACROSS_THE_OUTAGE` are untouched, and D195's sharpened assertion at 1179 is untouched with them. Red **10 of 10** before and green **10 of 10** after at the same condition; green **20 of 20** under the load that gave the old test the formerly-red arm 11 of 20, reaching regime 1 in all 20. Regimes 2 and 3 were driven on purpose against the fixed oracle: green 5 of 5 with `holding` 1 in two of them, and red 5 of 5 at 1156 with CI's own message, which is the right answer. **What is left is a bounded race, not an impossibility, and it must be recorded that way:** E-21 stops the written-off originals at the redial (`herd-runner/src/lib.rs:2051`), so one ordering remains — the second attempt must reach `take_job` before the stopped originals' processes are actually gone. It is structurally favourable, a `tokio::spawn` against a round trip plus a signal and a `/proc` sweep, and it was **never lost in 35 measured runs** of the fixed test, 20 of them on two pinned CPUs under eight spinners; no test figure moves it. The strong claim that **is** true: the regime is no longer produced by **scheduling on the dispatch path**, which is what made the outcome a matter of luck on whatever machine ran it. A record claiming impossibility would send the next reader looking in the wrong place the day it goes red again. If it ever bites, the shape that removes it is a hand-written runner against the real hub (`Puppet` in `herd-hub/tests/link.rs` can send `AttemptAbandoned` with `at_its_ceiling` directly), at the cost of no longer proving that a real runner produces the refusal. **The survey this finding spawned is ORCH-160, and it is open.** | — | `history/2026-10-27-orch-107-contention-timeouts.md` *Risks and findings*; `herd-hub/tests/announced_job_ceiling.rs:1179`; CI runs `40720983497` (red, `main`, `7eb9585`) and `40719469672` (green, same content); `history/2026-10-28-orch-119-announced-ceiling.md`; **D195**; ORCH-126; CI run `40857453150` (red, `main`, `b73d38c`, the third consecutive red, at 1156); `history/2026-10-29-orch-119-clockless-oracle.md`; **D208**; `herd-hub/tests/announced_job_ceiling.rs` (the two fill loops now wait on `JobState::Running`); `herd-hub/src/server.rs:4144`; ORCH-160; CI run `40870735400` (green, `main`, `6036d24`, the streak broken, read at batch 4's landing); CI run `40882112506` (green, `main`, `171c780`, **the second consecutive green**, read by `herd-merger` at batch 5's landing -- the run batch 4 recorded for the next landing to carry, `conclusion: success`, jobs `check` and `docs` both success); CI run `40896526152` (green, `main`, `10ff31d`, **the third consecutive green**, read by `herd-merger` at batch 6's landing -- the run batch 5 recorded for the next landing to carry, `conclusion: success`, `status: completed`); CI run `40908071577` (green, `main`, `cd0f1b7`, **the fourth consecutive green**, read by `herd-merger` at batch 7's landing -- the run batch 6 recorded for the next landing to carry, `conclusion: success`. | ORCH-163 | **The hub never records a failed resume and re-asks for ever.** The first consumer's trace: the hub asked for the files of one job on 10-24 at 10:23:06Z, the runner answered *"this runner holds no collection for it"* at 12:49:13Z and again at 13:06:31Z, again after their 10-26 reboot, and the hub container shows six more asks. **The runner's answer goes to its own stderr and no frame goes to the hub.** `Session::files_held`'s `other` arm (`herd-runner/src/lib.rs:1689-1699`) `eprintln!`s and returns `false`, and no `ToHub` variant means "I do not hold it", so the hub has nothing to record and nothing to stop re-asking on. **The protocol finding is what makes this cheap: no version bump is needed.** An optional `#[serde(default)]` field on an existing frame variant is the one change allowed to leave `PROTOCOL_VERSION` and the hub's record version alone, written into `herd-proto/src/lib.rs` at `18a1c82` for `JobMetrics::pressure` and governed by **D73** — and before M16 both numbers are free-running anyway, so a bump would be harmless rather than forbidden. **Correction to the precedent as briefed, measured at this landing:** `load_milli` is *not* that precedent. `ToHub::Health::load_milli` arrived at `7ca9597`, the same commit that moved `PROTOCOL_VERSION` from 3 to 4; what `135510e` added without a bump was `JobMetrics::host_load_milli`, a record field and not a frame field. Use `18a1c82` and D73. **And the correction that matters the other way:** a *new* `ToHub` variant would break, but **not at serde** — `ToHub` carries `#[serde(other)] Unknown` at `herd-proto/src/lib.rs:1730-1731` — it would break at the hub's relay catch-all, `herd-hub/src/server.rs:4468-4472`, which answers `FromHub::Refused { reason: "a runner may not send …" }` and `return Ok(())`, dropping the connection | `collect-gap` diagnosis §1 and §6, 2026-10-29, from the first consumer's own trace; row written and the two protocol claims re-measured by `herd-merger` at batch 6's landing | herd-planner, then herd-step | open | Size it, then brief it as two halves that can land separately: (a) the runner says what it holds, as an optional field on an existing `ToHub` frame — no new variant, no version bump; (b) the hub records a resume it asked for and was answered negatively, and stops re-asking for ever. **Do not widen the frame set to do (a)** | the specification question in `history/2026-10-29-collect-gap.md` *Decisions needed* 1 — whether `finished / exited 0` over an incomplete collection is a defect or a choice about `finished` — which is Alexa's, is **not yet a row**, and is flagged in `history/2026-10-29-merge-batch-6.md` *Not done* | `history/2026-10-29-collect-gap.md` §1, §6 and *Decisions needed*; `history/2026-10-29-merge-batch-6.md`; `herd-runner/src/lib.rs:1689-1699`; `herd-proto/src/lib.rs:1730-1731`, `18a1c82`, `7ca9597`, `135510e`; `herd-hub/src/server.rs:4468-4472`; **D19**, **D73**, **D148**; ORCH-165, ORCH-166 |

| ORCH-246 | **Has a requester any silence bound at all? 95 `herdctl` processes were measured idle for 5 h 36 m, and the question underneath is whether that is a leak or a missing bound.** Measured by the orchestrator at 08:45 on 2026-10-30: **95 processes, our uid, all in `Sl` state, 69.3 MiB total, oldest idle 20339 s**. **Whose they were is measured now, and this row's first answer was wrong:** they were **the consumer's production nightly requesters** — `herdctl wait --outputs-to /mnt/ssd_980/chatons/agents/nightly/herd-j<id> --verdict-to <path>` — read off `ps -o args` an hour later. uid **1002** (`server`) carries their nightly and our own test fixtures alike, and `comm` is `herdctl` for both, so *our uid* and *nobody living started these* prove nothing about ownership. **The error is the orchestrator's**, not any agent's. **Filed as a question with its measurement and deliberately not as a defect**, because the measurement admits two readings and nothing yet separates them: either **a requester has no silence bound**, where both the runner and the hub do (E-21, and the watchdog), and a `herdctl` whose hub went away will wait for ever; or a test harness killed its hub without reaping its clients, which is a harness defect and says nothing about `herdctl`. **Correction, 2026-10-30, and it is the orchestrator's: not one of them exited on its own.** Every one was signalled. **78** were killed by the orchestrator between 08:45 and 09:00, `SIGTERM` by explicit PID; **the rest by the consumer at 09:31**, on Alexa's request — `TERM` on the loop's whole process group, **pgid 256928, 415 processes** — and then `herdctl cancel` on its **126** open nightly jobs (125 queued, one running, `j0000003749`): **126 cancelled, 0 failures**, with the runner confirming *"ending j0000003749 because a requester cancelled this job"*. So a later census finding **zero** of them is not a second data point on whether a requester exits by itself, and reading it as one would weaken this hypothesis for a reason that never happened. Batch 18's merger reported what it could see and said in the same breath that it could not tell *exited on their own* from *something reaped them* — it was right to, and **it had not been told of the kills**. **The second reading above is refuted by the same measurement:** these were a production loop's requesters and not a test harness's clients, so no harness failed to reap them. **The evidence that does bear on the hypothesis, measured before the kill:** all 78 were in state **`Sl`** — alive and blocked, **not `Z`** — and the 71 measured later had burned **0 seconds of CPU between them**, the oldest over 5 h 30 m old. Alive, blocked, no CPU is consistent with a blocking wait that has **no timeout**, and is the opposite of an unreaped zombie. **A third reading this opens, stated and not resolved:** the consumer's hub (PID **3006543**) was up since 20:23 and was never touched that morning, and **125 of the 126 jobs were still queued** — so these requesters were blocked on jobs that had not ended, which is `herdctl wait` keeping its promise rather than missing a bound. The *hub went away* case the hypothesis is about was never the case here, which is one more reason the count settles nothing. | measured by the orchestrator, 2026-10-30 08:45; filed by `herd-merger` at batch 18's landing; **corrected on `shared-host-and-selectors`, 2026-10-30**, on the orchestrator's instruction and with its own figures | herd-step | open | **Verify before asserting, and read `herdctl`'s own link handling first.** The cheap discriminator is whether a `herdctl` whose hub is killed exits on its own within a bounded time: one hub, one `herdctl` following a job, kill the hub by PID, time the client. If it never exits, this is a missing bound beside E-21's and the row becomes a defect with a named invariant behind it; if it exits, there is no missing bound here and these 95 need no explanation beyond the one the correction gives — they were blocked on jobs that had not ended. **Do not reason from the idle count alone** — a stock on a shared host measures accumulated history, not today's behaviour. **That this row would kill none of them is overtaken by events, not reversed:** they are gone and every one was signalled (the correction opposite), and the *may anyone* question was answered on 2026-10-30 by Alexa asking the consumer to stop its own loop. What stands untouched is the rule the morning cost — *kill only PIDs you started*, and establish that from a process's full `args`, because uid, `comm`, age and PID are not provenance (ORCH-249). **And the discriminator is not to be run from a prose branch:** it starts a hub and a client and belongs to a step with a brief. | E-21; the watchdog | `ps` census of 2026-10-30 08:45 (95 procs, `Sl`, 69.3 MiB, oldest 20339 s) and the `ps -o args` read an hour later; the 78 killed by explicit PID between 08:45 and 09:00; the consumer's `TERM` on pgid 256928 (415 processes) at 09:31 and its 126 cancellations with 0 failures; E-21 in `docs/coverage.md`; `history/2026-10-30-merge-batch-18.md`, its *Host* note on the 95 and the hub PID 3006543 up since 20:23; ORCH-249 |
| ORCH-247 | **A requester cannot ask which of its jobs are open, so an operator read Herd's state root to find 126 ids to cancel.** The consumer's own finding, from real use on the morning of 2026-10-30, reported through their orchestrator in their words: « le hub ne propose pas de lister les jobs d'un requester ou d'une priorité. J'ai dû lire la racine en lecture seule pour trouver les 126 identifiants à annuler. Un `herdctl list --priority nightly --state queued,running` (ou un `cancel --all-of <priorité>`) aurait suffi. » **Their figures, as they reported them:** 126 open nightly jobs — 125 queued and one running, `j0000003749` — all cancelled, **0 failures**, after a `TERM` on the loop's process group (pgid 256928, 415 processes), with the runner confirming *"ending j0000003749 because a requester cancelled this job"*. **So cancellation itself worked exactly as promised and this row is about the missing surface and nothing else.** **What the tree says, measured at `3f3a07c`:** `herdctl` submits, follows and cancels **one** job by id — `Wait` at `herdctl/src/main.rs:584` and `Cancel` at `:596`, each taking a `job_id` — and `Action` has no jobs `list` at all, while `herdctl runner list` does exist (`:649`), so a listing is not categorically foreign to this CLI. **`herd-hub status` does not close it, and why is the useful part:** the status document carries `running` and `queued` with each job's id, priority and labels (`herd-hub/src/status.rs:155`-`:157`, `RunningNow` at `:328`, `QueuedNow` at `:349`), so the *information* exists — but it is a file in the state root (`status.json`, `STATUS_VERSION` 1, E-35, D215) read from the operator's side of the fence, which is precisely what the consumer had to do. **And it could not be narrowed to one requester even if a requester could read it:** nothing on the record, on the status document or on `ToHub::SubmitJob` names who submitted a job, and Q8 gives **one shared requester token** (`herd-hub/src/main.rs:341`), so *its own work* is not expressible in this build | the consumer, from real use on 2026-10-30, reported through their orchestrator and quoted here in their words; measured against the tree at `3f3a07c` by `shared-host-and-selectors`, 2026-10-30 | herd-planner | open | **Name the authority question instead of letting a flag settle it, and say what makes this surface legitimate at all.** Three shapes, stated and deliberately not chosen. **(a)** `herdctl list` with `--priority` and `--state queued,running`: a read, and the cheapest. **(b)** `herdctl cancel --all-of <priority>`: the convenience they actually asked for, and it carries the hard half — cancelling by selector lets one requester cancel jobs it did not submit, which is **M15's token-and-level territory (E-48, Q8)** and not a CLI flag's to decide, the more so while the token is shared and *its own* has no meaning. **(c)** Neither, and the gap is declared to be the operator's screen: `herd-hub status` already carries the queue and the running jobs, and the answer is that a requester asks about one job at a time while the fleet is read from the state root — a ruling, not a feature. **Weigh all three against invariant 1:** a listing with filters is close to the generic-CI surface we refuse, and what distinguishes this one is a requester asking about **its own** work, which `herdctl` already answers one id at a time — so the legitimate form of (a) is *my* jobs, which **needs per-requester identity and therefore sits behind M15, not in front of it**. **This step's lean, and it is a lean:** (c) for v1.0.0 with the answer written where the consumer will look for it, (a) scoped to a requester's own jobs once M15 gives identity, and (b) last, being the one shape that can destroy another requester's work | the consumer reads `status.json` or runs `herd-hub status` against the state root, as it did on 2026-10-30 | the consumer's report of 2026-10-30 (126 open nightly jobs, 125 queued, one running `j0000003749`, 0 failures, pgid 256928, 415 processes); | ORCH-092 | **Startup sweep for the runner's output spool.** A streamed collection's bytes live in `{data root}/outputs/{job}-{attempt}` until the hub says it holds every file, and `files_acknowledged` (`herd-runner/src/lib.rs:1539`) is the **only** code that removes one — it needs the process that produced the collection to still hold it in memory, so a restart stranded the spool for ever with nothing in the tree even enumerating it. The first consumer reports 125 spools holding 65 GB, all under 7 days old | the consumer's report of 2026-10-26, brief of 2026-10-27 | herd-step (done), herd-merger (integration) | done | **Integrated 2026-10-27 by `herd-merger`** in the ORCH-080/ORCH-092 batch, the first batch under Alexa's *"une PR = plusieurs lots"* of the same day; merged under ORCH-001 on green CI. The runner sweeps `outputs/` at startup, re-registers each spool under the existing offer-and-acknowledge path (no protocol change, `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 2, no hub behaviour change), and **sets aside — never deletes — what no hub claims**; whether it may ever delete is **ORCH-108**, with Alexa. **Three things it establishes rather than reads.** (1) What the hub does with a **re-offered collection it already holds**: `store.rs:1851-1856` in `write_output_chunk` returns the collection's state rather than refusing or rewriting, so one re-offered chunk completes it and `JobFilesAck` comes back. The author first asserted the opposite, expecting a refusal, and it failed loudly — **the failing-first assertion is still in the file, worded as the one that decides the design**, and it is the evidence rather than a leftover. (2) It **refutes the cancelled-job hypothesis as it was stated**: `cancel_job` (`server.rs:6667`) writes no end, the runner still collects, spools and sends `JobFinished`, and `collection_owed` (`store.rs:2389`) never looks at whether a job was cancelled — so **a cancel alone does not orphan a spool while the runner stays up**. What orphans one is any end whose bytes have not all crossed when the **runner is restarted**. (3) **A hazard it closes:** nothing gated a collection against a hub whose **state root is gone**, though the verdict path already did, and the consequence is worse than a refusal — `files_may_land` (`server.rs:4347`) checks only the attempt number and the runner name, so where those line up one job's output could be written onto another job's record. It composes with id re-use, which is why **#97 and this change together close that pair**: a recreated state root used to mint `j0000000001` again, so an old spool's `{job}-{attempt}` could match a brand-new job. **Not touched:** how `Inner::collections` is keyed. That is **ORCH-065**, still open — D177 and PR #85 re-keyed `Inner::jobs` by `(job, attempt)` and left `collections: HashMap<JobId, FilesOut>` alone (`lib.rs:888`, `insert` at `:1479`), verified on `main` at `c831d55`. ## Next

**Alexa's framing, 2026-10-27, and it sets the order of everything after:
finish the remediation first, then the features that were expected.** The
first consumer is updating Herd and its development is resuming, so what
it will need from us in the near term is **support more than new
features** — answering version questions, reading reports that carry a
job id, and fixing what their use finds. Nothing on their side is ours
to touch (D161).

1. **Three answers from Alexa, 2026-10-27, *"1) oui 2) oui 3) oui
   obsolete"*.** **ORCH-075 — yes**, written up as **D183**: the keeper
   is the target architecture, the durability tier is taken in three
   steps, and **step A alone is authorised** (ORCH-083). B (ORCH-084)
   and C (ORCH-085) are not adopted by that answer, and **nothing is
   dropped before ORCH-079 and ORCH-081 are done**. **ORCH-068 — yes**:
   the live launcher `/mnt/ssd_980/herd/claude/env.sh` is authoritative
   and the shipped `ops/agents/env.sh` is aligned on it; the row is
   `done`, PR #93, repository side only. **PR #67 — obsolete**,
   closed unmerged the same day; D170 stays its gap (D148).
2. **C5 is finished** (ORCH-060): parts 1, 2 and 3 are on `main` as PRs
   #83, #84 and #85, carrying D177, D178 and D180-D182. Invariant 2 is
   now enforced at the runner as well as at the hub — a runner holds its
   own ceiling, announces it, and a refusal at that ceiling spends none
   of the job's goes.
3. **The ordered task list is ORCH-079 to ORCH-090.** **ORCH-079 is
   `done`**: the D103 ordering oracle is on `main` (PR #95), it is the
   regression test ORCH-083 and ORCH-084 have to keep green, and it is
   the first piece of the dossier to change a file under `herd-hub/src`
   — nine lines, to observe and never to alter. **ORCH-081 is `done`**
   too (PR #96, **D184**): `SIGTERM` no longer kills the hub, and with
   it ORCH-083's last open dependency is cleared. **ORCH-080 is `done`**
   as well (PR #92): 308 crash points built rather than crashed, and
   **B's hazard is exactly 4 of them**, which is the split ORCH-084 has
   to keep. **ORCH-082 is `done`** as well (PR #104, 2026-10-27), so
   **all four of ORCH-079 to ORCH-082 are done** and none of the four is
   still open — the sentence this item carried until that integration.
   The store's lock no longer spans a record read; the gain is
   **prospective**, eight callers going ×8.49 → ×1.71 against a lockless
   control of ×1.66 while **one caller saves nothing**, and it is the
   convoy `05 §4.3` names that it keeps the keeper from inheriting. Two
   of that row's own findings are now **ORCH-123** (per-record CPU, the
   larger lever) and **ORCH-124** (a permanent budget leak).
   **ORCH-087, the keeper, has its dependency met and is not started:**
   D183 adopted it as the direction, not as an open worksite, so opening
   it is Alexa's word. Then the tier, one step at a time, A first and
   only A authorised.
4. **Six rows wait on Alexa, four of them arrived together**
   (PR #94, 2026-10-27): **ORCH-105** decision A, whether the shipped
   seven-day output floor stays with the hub saying what it can really
   keep or the default drops; **ORCH-095** decision B, the
   `--volume-free-floor` knob and whether a second number is the
   beginning of a policy language; **ORCH-098** decision C, the
   retention class map 90/14; and **ORCH-103**, a relay rather than a
   decision — one per-directory listing on the consumer's host that
   would settle H1, H2 and H3, which nobody here may run (D161). They
   are one at a time, and **A first**: it is the only one of the four
   that changes what is shipped. The fifth is **ORCH-106**, from
   ORCH-080's enumeration (PR #92) and written at the same integration:
   a torn `job.json` makes the hub refuse the whole state root and exit
   before binding a port, where everything else in the store degrades
   loudly. **It outranks all four**, because it is live on the
   consumer's machine today and nobody there can repair it (D161), and
   it must be settled before ORCH-085. **The sixth is ORCH-108**, from
   ORCH-092 (the batch of 2026-10-27): may a runner ever remove an
   `.unclaimed` spool by itself, or does it stay until a human does?
   Recommended: the human, until one real sweep says how much of the
   65 GB is recoverable. ORCH-076 is answered, and
   answered **by measurement rather than by his word**: the hub volume
   and the runner data root are both on `/mnt/ssd_980`, ext4 on NVMe,
   983 GB with ~341 free, reported from their end and matched by `df`
   here minutes later. It confirms ORCH-053 from the other direction.
   **The consequence is a constraint, not a reassurance:** that
   filesystem is **shared** — their hub volume and runner data root,
   chatOnS's caches and this project's agent build trees compete for the
   same free space — so how many heavy agents run here is now bounded by
   disk as well as by CPU. The hub's disk policy is derived from it and
   is now on `main`: ORCH-093, `docs/disk-policy.md`, with its work as
   rows ORCH-095 to ORCH-105 above.
5. Then the owed rows that nothing blocks: ORCH-048 (the skip counters
   over-count), ORCH-061 (the criterion the S3 brief must carry),
   ORCH-015, ORCH-016, and ORCH-091, which is now only the
   dossier index's numbering note.
6. **The suite is green on this workstation for the first time**, and
   what was red was this host and not the code. **ORCH-069 is done**
   (2026-10-27): both its assertions declared a lend larger than the
   cgroup enclosing the runner leaves free, so the runner clamped it
   (D119) and the hub's sentences carried the clamped figure. Both
   fixtures now declare 2 GiB, which no host able to build this
   workspace can clamp, and `slots.rs` skips nothing anywhere — on CI
   it was **skipped**, never a pass (D27). Measured again on the merged
   tree at batch 4: **837 passed, 0 failed, 12 skipped**, the twelve
   all cgroup delegation, and that is a count and not a floor because
   the run completed (D179). **ORCH-107 is `done` too** (batch 4), and
   what it fixed is not what the row predicted: the `real_herd` tests
   built the binaries they drive **inside their own `PATIENCE`**, and
   `scripts/test.sh` now runs that exact build first, advisory. **The
   advice this board gave for it was wrong** — `cargo build --workspace
   --all-targets` resolves a narrower feature set than `cargo test
   --workspace`, so it left 9.7 s of compiling inside the test process;
   only the identical command removes it, and `test.sh` now does it for
   everyone. `PATIENCE` is still 30 s and nothing became a skip; a wait
   that runs out says whether it waited on the product or on rustc.
   **What is not explained is the 21 failures at load 19**: four runs at
   peak loads 9.6 to 18.2 gave **zero** `:649`/`:789` timeouts, and that
   event had three full suites at once, which was deliberately not
   reproduced on the host the consumer's hub runs on. No row may upgrade
   that residue to "fixed". One loaded run found something else, which
   is **ORCH-119**. **The clamp behind ORCH-069 is still proved by no
   test that runs here** (ORCH-116).
7. **A skip count is a per-host figure** (ORCH-070) **and a per-load
   one** (ORCH-107, 2026-10-27): 12 on this workstation at rest, **14**
   here under load — `flood-fifty` and `flood-two-fifty` skip themselves
   above a one-minute load of 8 — and **14** on CI's `check` since
   ORCH-069, where it was 15 and the one that left is `slots.rs`'s
   derived `-j`, which CI run 40535627242 **runs and passes** instead of
   skipping; 14 confirmed again on run 40538722315. So a reader must
   compare the **list** and never the number. Until 2026-10-28 that was
   the reason the local run was not the gate; it is now a rule **of**
   the gate — `scripts/gate.sh` compares the list and is silent on the
   count (**ORCH-120** answered, **D187**, item 9). ORCH-071 is
   done (PR #88, **D179**): an incomplete or narrowed run now says so
   instead of reporting no skips. ORCH-048, the over-counting in the
   same two scripts, is untouched by it.
8. **The durability tier has begun: step A is on `main`** (ORCH-083,
   **D183**, PR #102). `record_waiting`'s `waiting` line no longer
   waits for the disk; **one event line of thirteen is unsynced and
   the other twelve still sync**, the record keeps both its syncs, and
   the store's own waiting write went **3.10 ms → 2.10 ms, 32.3 %**
   against the ~31 % predicted. Two things came with it and neither is
   settled: the `--durable-waits` flag **D183 itself names** was not
   built (**ORCH-113**, with Alexa), and the wider reading — every
   event line unsynced — is **ORCH-114**, to be asked together with
   step B (ORCH-084) and not before.
9. **The pull request has left the loop, and two of the transition's
   three steps are done** (**ORCH-120** answered 2026-10-28, **D187**,

   une option, mais une obligation."* `herd-merger` alone pushes `main`,

   method and the deletion of `.github/pull_request_template.md`.
   **Step 3 is done** — the first local landing, 2026-10-28, which
   deleted `ci.yml`'s `pull_request:` trigger, the one lane that could,
   since a pull request removing that trigger can never produce the two
   contexts it requires. **`ci.yml` now runs only on a push to `main`.**
   **Step 2 is closed, 2026-10-29, and not by being done** (**D203**):
   branch protection and `enforce_admins` are **deliberately not
   pursued**, and that is the accepted final state rather than a gap.
   First-hand evidence from the first local landing — GitHub printed
   `Bypassed rule violations … Changes must be made through a pull
   request … 2 of 2 required status checks are expected` and let the push

   nothing. **GitHub is storage.** What guards `main` is `gate.sh`,
   `pre-push` and the contract. Nothing is pending on the transition now.

12. **The hub answers its pack frames, and D231 decision 5 is still
    owed** — batch 15, 2026-10-30, one branch (`pack-wire`):
    `JobStore::packs()` is the seam the hub's loop reaches a registry
    through, with a default body of `None` so that no implementor and no
    test double had to move; six pack `ToHub` frames are dispatched
    behind two named arrival-window predicates, where all six used to
    fall through to `other =>` and be answered `FromHub::Refused`, which
    closes the link — so a runner that announced a holding lost its
    connection; and `Hello.mesh_endpoint` is kept rather than discarded,
    without which every holding recorded would carry `endpoint: None`
    and say **outbound-only** of a runner that had advertised an address
    (**D247**). **What it deliberately did not do** is loosen seven
    tests in two files it was not pointed at in order to turn a gate
    green: `FromHub::ReadPacksAgain` is constructed nowhere, and the
    product change was withdrawn on its own measurement — 6 failures of

    done` and not `done`**: a hub *told* about a holding records it with
    the endpoint its handshake advertised and forgets it when the link
    goes, **measured against a hand-driven peer and nothing measured
    against the real runner binary**, and the runner neither answers an
    ask nor announces unprompted. The three rows that follow are

    needs no hub change and makes the substance true from the side that
